8. **Components:** every dependency of the table of 10.1 is met; no settings section is present without its component; the checks of 10.1 that are not set dependencies hold (`[peer]` when the hot store runs and the membership document names more than one node; `[fuse]` only on Linux; `[nfs]` only on macOS); `[client]` names exactly one of `seeds` and `membership`; at least one component runs. The node configuration refuses keys it does not know (a `role` key among them), unlike the membership document, which ignores them (10.8). The keys of the node cache, fetch engine, fronts, Prepare provider and profiles are in the build-node example of 21.2.

### 10.15 Capacity for our hardware (h)
All figures [E] from the stated hardware and marks; decimal TB.

**Hot tier (3 x 6.4 TB U.2 NVMe).**

| Quantity | Value |
|---|---|
| Raw NVMe | 19.2 TB (17.5 TiB) |
| Usable (logical) at `hot.replicas = 3` | 6.4 TB (`replicas = 2` would give 9.6 TB) |
| Destage start / urgent / slow / reject (of raw, dirty) | 9.6 / 14.4 / 16.3 / 17.3 TB raw: 3.2 / 4.8 / 5.4 / 5.8 TB logical at `replicas = 3` |
| Dirty room between start and reject | 2.6 TB logical: about 75 days of 34 GB/day, about 3.8 days at 20x |
| NVMe write rate from ingest | 3 x 34 GB/day = 102 GB/day cluster-wide, 34 GB/day per device (plus destage reads and promotion writes) |
| Promoted copies (cache segments, single copy, clean, reclaimable) | at most 20% of raw: 3.84 TB |
| Index | in the embedded store on each node's NVMe (10.2); 1.39 M keys today; T-S3 measures bytes per key and cache size at 1e8 keys |

At today's ingest destage is driven by age (`destage.max_age`), not by the marks: the marks and reclaim order are first exercised above about 1.1 TB/day (3.2 TB of logical dirty bytes within the 3-day window) [E].

**Cold tier (45 x 6 TB SAS = 270 TB = 245.6 TiB raw).** `cold.fill_limit` 85% gives 229.5 TB raw (208.7 TiB).

| Code | Overhead | Logical capacity at the fill limit | **Logical capacity under the capacity guard** (`node_loss_reserve`: 0.85 x (2 x 90 - 2 x 6) = 142.8 TB raw) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `replicas = 2` | 2.0x | 114.8 TB | 71.4 TB |
| `replicas = 3` | 3.0x | 76.5 TB | 47.6 TB |
| `4+2` | 1.5x | 153.0 TB | 95.2 TB |
| **`4+4` (default)** | 2.0x | 114.8 TB | **71.4 TB** |
| `5+4` | 1.8x | 127.5 TB | 79.3 TB |

The guard keeps free, on the two nodes that would survive a node loss, the lost node's share plus one drive per survivor: after the node-loss rebuild of 10.4 the survivors are at about 79% of their raw, below `fill_limit`, with a drive's worth of space each for the next drive failure.

**Horizon at `4+4` under the guard (71.4 TB):** 1.34 TB is live today (1.9%); at 34 GB/day (which includes today's dead bytes) the guard is reached in about 5.7 years; at 20x today's rate (about 680 GB/day) in about 105 days; GC (10.5) lowers the rate of growth, and Q1's switch to `4+2`, which the operator makes once logical use passes half of the guarded capacity (about 36 TB), raises the ceiling to 95.2 TB. Fill is also checked against the measured live set and ingest by T0-F C-6 before the code is frozen: the capacity gap of silo (raw about 4.6x logical) is unexplained and does not enter this table.

**Rates.** Aggregate cold read bandwidth is about 45 x 190 MB/s = 8.5 GB/s of spindle against about 9.4 GB/s of network for three 25 Gbit/s storage nodes [I]; with one storage node down it is about 2.8 GB/s, because a third of the reads are degraded at `k = 4` reads each on 30 drives (4.3). Today's read load is 0.5 MB/s [M] and ingest 0.4 MB/s, so neither bound is near; they set the ceilings the controller of 4.3 works under. A stripe of `4+4` is 8 GiB of sequential writes spread over 8 drives, a few seconds at spindle rate.


## 11. The cache device: a real partition, not a loop on an image
**Decision [D]: a dedicated xfs partition of the node SSD (noatime), no loop device. Required, not gated:** T0 measured that the loop device turns `O_DIRECT` cache writes into page-cache writes (945+ MB/s that have not reached the SSD) and stalls four buffered writers for more than 20 s [M]. The other reasons stand:
1. Each `fdatasync` through xfs-on-loop-on-image costs two journals and two flushes.
2. Two page caches and two dirty accountings double memory pressure (`losetup --direct-io=on` was the considered stopgap; with `O_DIRECT` already defeated by the loop it is not relied on).
3. Cell-group eviction frees space only if discards propagate through the loop to the image; on a partition `PUNCH_HOLE` frees directly and `fstrim` works.
4. A fixed 185 GiB image takes the space whether or not the cache is full.
5. One fewer layer between FUSE reads and the SSD.

The device is the build node's local SSD that holds the cache (on the farm's six existing nodes the root SATA SSD; their SMR drives are never a cache): a new partition of budget + 15% (185 GiB -> 220 GiB). The 31 build nodes of the target farm have their own cache SSDs, whose size and type T0 records. **If the root filesystem is xfs it cannot be shrunk; T0 records its type.** The plan is free unallocated SSD space if it exists, else a node image re-provision through hostkeeper in a maintenance window; never an in-place live repartition by tierstore.

**T0 B** measures the partition (it is no longer a gate): `fdatasync` p99, sustained write at 1 MiB and mixed-load read p99, against the loop numbers above. **T10b depends on T13 so constants are frozen on the shipped device.** The partition is mounted at a new path read from `cache.root`; the old image is not converted and is removed after the soak.

**Metrics added by revision 8, not yet in the table above.** The table is the registry of `tierstore-metrics` (its test holds the two equal both ways), so each name below joins the table in the pull request that registers it, by the task that implements it: `tierstore_components{component}` (gauge: which components a node runs, 10.1; the daemon, T-L); `membership_epoch` (gauge), `membership_fetch_total{outcome}` (counter: ok, not_newer, invalid, not_found, unauthorized, unreachable) and `membership_bootstrap_seconds` (histogram) of the client's membership (10.8; T-SB); `hedges_suppressed_total{reason}` (counter: budget, decrease, ceiling, no_alternative), `locate_local_total` and `peer_load_pings_total` (counters) of the hedge budget and the no-ping rules (4.6, 10.6; T3, T-S6); `first_touch_total{ready}` (counter: each first read of a cell in a job session, `ready = yes` if it was `present`) and `first_read_seconds` (histogram: from the provider's `Prepare` to the job's first served READ), which decide whether Prepare's order matters (6.1, 17.3; T-F8 and the Prepare provider); and the operations metrics of 24.10 (T-S13, T-S16).

`request_to_start_seconds` (tierstore's `tierstore_request_to_start_seconds`, T14) is one sample per run, on the build node's clock: from the first `Prepare` of the run that the mount provider takes (the scheduler's agent sends it the moment it has the job) to the first lookup or listing the mount serves inside one of the run's trees (the agent and the overlay's mount touch only the roots, so that is the container runtime making the job's root or the command itself), which the mount reports on the fill socket off the request's path. `{drive}` is the drive id (45 hard drives and 3 NVMe devices), never a key. Trace each demand read end to end: handler, wait sent, budget acquired, `LOCATE`, device chosen, GET start, device queue wait, first byte, cell received, hash done, write, reply.

## 13. Deleted code and migration of the cache
**Deleted**
- FUSE passthrough (`treefs/backing.rs`, `core/passthrough.rs`, `Handle::Backed` and `Spilled`, `max_backing_ids`, backing table, negotiation in `fuse.rs::init`).
- The per-blob path: `Plan::Blob`, `Origin::Span`, `fill_blob`, the `fill <sha> <size> from <key> @ <off>` request, `blob_path` in `cache_file`, per-file span copies.
- `pin_once`, `pinning`, `lifecycle`, `loaded.pinned`, touch/pin/unpin/pins_live traffic.
- All three prefetch mechanisms: `local_cache/prefetch.rs`, `fs-prefetch` threads, Prepare groups and `GROUP_MAX_BYTES`.
- Extents machinery: `local_cache/extents/*`, per-range flights, `extent_save`, `extents.writing`, `move_whole`'s re-hash, `fetch_parts` gap reading.
- The v1 list reader (only refusal remains) and, after the dual-emission release, the CAS service's v1 list writer.
- The komira-ai/fuser fork dependency and the blocking mount client (T-R, T11a).
- The erasure-set budget model and the `upstream.data_shards`/`upstream.drives` topology flags (T11a); with silo, the legacy upstream and the `upload_part_size` handling (T11b; no silo client was ever written as an upstream).
- Config: `max_concurrent_fills`, `fill_socket_concurrency`, `prefetch_concurrency`, `trees.fill_concurrency`, `fill_chunk`, `trees.fill_queue`, `trees.fill_rate`; `trees.coalesce_gap` and `trees.whole_pack_percent` replaced by section 6.

**Kept:** the reserve and critical-mark check (at claim time), staging files and create-only link for non-pack objects, lock-generation `owns()` checks, the S3-front index, tree image serving (komira-ai/tierstore#341, komira-ai/tierstore#342), tree store placements and the provider Prepare entry point, the NFS front on macOS.

**Cache layout migration.** It is a cache; there is no conversion. The new binary writes `packs-v2/`, does not read `extents-v1/` or `origin=span` entries, and deletes them in a low-priority `tierstore gc legacy`. **Rollback:** the old binary ignores `packs-v2/`; during the overlap `packs-v2` is capped at 50% of the budget and the rollback procedure includes `tierstore gc packs-v2 --purge` (a held script) before the old binary starts. List-format rollback is in 3.5; store rollback is in 10.10.

## 14. Test plan
Behaviour tests only: an injected clock, a fake upstream with scripted per-part and per-cell completion order and corruption, a fake drive and a fake storage node with scripted service times, hangs, errors and crashes, scripted interleavings and message loss between nodes, and progress barriers. No test asserts timing: every "after X seconds" below is a step of the injected clock.

| Test | Proves | Defect it catches |
|---|---|---|
| N readers on spans of one pack: one `PackFill`; each cell claimed once | Single flight | Duplicate fills |
| Demand sub-range plus overlapping grid part: skips claimed cells; GETs <= parts + sub-ranges; bytes_fetched == bytes_needed | Claim state | Cell fetched twice |
| Reverse-order part completion: a read on the last cell is served after only that cell lands | Independent per-cell verification | Frontier stalls |
| A 1 GiB file span: a read at the end waits only for its cells | Per-cell digests | Whole-file wait |
| Hedge and original both deliver a cell: one publishes, the loser's bytes never written | Write-once cells | TOCTOU rewrite |
| Two lists with the same pack bytes give one PackId; a changed pack a new path | `pack_root` identity | Path collision |
| Ramp and grid: a first touch mid-pack ramps to the next grid boundary and every later part is a whole grid part; at offset 0 the 5th piece (4 MiB, after 4+8+16+32) is trimmed at the 64 MiB boundary; no runt after the ramp | 4.4 | Runt parts, boundary crossing |
| Budget: floor, share, ceiling and bytes in flight equal the table of 4.3 for (D, N, s*, P) and are recomputed when P changes; share is at least one part at every P | 4.3 | Contradictory budget |
| v1 list or missing L2 sidecar: refused, `ListVersionUnsupported`, no bytes served, no code path parses v1 | No legacy reader | Serving unparsed data |
| Backfill: `l2-backfill` writes an L2 sidecar under the unchanged tree id, reads each pack once, and a second run writes nothing | 3.5 | Identity change; repeated reads |
| Dual emission: the CAS service writes v1 and L2 under one unchanged tree id; old reader ignores the sidecar; new reader ignores v1; id equals the pre-change id for the same tree | 3.5 | Action-cache invalidation |
| The CAS service on `ListVersionUnsupported` uses its miss path and the job continues | 3.5 | Partial outage |
| L2 round trip: each cell digest equals independent sha256; deterministic; sorted directories | Format | Wrong digests |
| Crash between `pwrite` and journal append: cell absent after restart; no journal record visible without its data | Commit ordering | Trusting zeros |
| Crash after a batched PUNCH fsync and before/after the punch: replay never marks a punched cell `present`; one fsync per batch | Section 8 | Zeros after punch + crash |
| Process crash, same boot: restored cells `present`, no recheck; different boot or no boot_id: every restored cell `recheck`, not served by the mount, corrupted cell refetched | Section 8 | Needless rehash; silent bad serve |
| Journal batching: at most one `fdatasync` per dirty entry per interval; `durable` set only after it | Batching | Per-part commit; DONTNEED on dirty pages |
| Loom: reader/evictor protocol with per-cell CAS, neighbouring cells going `absent -> claimed -> present` during the scan, a reader straddling groups, post-read recheck: no reader returns bytes of a cell whose punch has begun; the evictor never changes a `claimed` cell | 5.1 | Zeros served; lost claim |
| Mount's state mapping is read-only: a `PROT_WRITE` mmap of the passed state memfd and a `write(2)` fail | 5.1 | Cross-mount corruption |
| Mount killed (lifeline hangup): counters dropped, eviction proceeds; mount whose protocol connection flaps keeps counters, regions and holds and is never zeroed while its lifeline is open | 5.1 | Wedged eviction; punch under a live reader |
| A mount grows its counter memfd past the server's mapping: the server remaps before acknowledging `open-pack`, and the evictor reads the new region's counters | 5.1 | Evictor reading past its mapping; a missed reader |
| Reader with a forced loss of its counter: post-read recheck discards the bytes | 5.1 | Zeros after forced clear |
| Live reader stuck beyond `evict_wait`: won cells restored `present`, nothing journaled, `evict_timeouts_total` increments | Evictor timeout | Evictor deadlock |
| More than 128 holders on one pack are all served | 5.1 | EBUSY on shared packs |
| Eviction removes an unheld entry's path while a mount holds the data fd: reads return that entry's bytes | fd, not path | Unlink race |
| Held-only cache: punch eviction admits a new job; admission refuses when estimate exceeds 0.9 x budget | Holds and admission | Deadlock, double counting |
| Scripted 2x first-byte or drive queue wait with every node at its floor: the ladder steps down and in-flight decreases; a `503` is a decrease signal for its drive | 4.3 | Floor removes authority |
| Stall before the first byte is not retried below the pre-first-byte timeout; in a decrease state only transport errors and `stalled` retry; one stall retry per drive per window; a retry goes to the other hot replica or a degraded read | 4.6 | Retry storm |
| Demand reserve granted with prepare saturating the node | Demand reserve | Starvation |
| Two jobs both at rank 0 alternate by DRR; small early-rank pack precedes the tail of a 4 GiB pack; active-pack window <= 3 | Fairness, rank | Head-of-line |
| Wait coalescing: at most one outstanding `wait` per 8 cells | Wait coalescing | Message storm |

| **NOW to LATER migration (scripted membership epochs on fake nodes): at every step every object reads correctly; nothing is placed on a `drain` hot device or drive; new PUTs go only to the three storage nodes at `replicas = 3`; every `4+2` stripe ends as `4+4` at 3/3/2 on the storage nodes with its block ids and offsets unchanged, so no index entry is rewritten; marking the combined nodes `in` again before the last step restores them as targets; a node whose configuration disagrees with the membership's cluster settings refuses to start** | 21.3, 10.8 | A migration that needs a rewrite; data placed on a device being emptied |
| `digest=none` object: the record or cell crc detects a flipped cell on the serving node; the client uses the other replica or degraded read; the copy is quarantined and repaired | 9, 10.3, 10.4 | Bitrot without silo |

**Why the failure and boundary list is complete for the storage tier.** Each state a drive or node can be in (`up`, `indexing`, `stalled`, `suspect`, `down`, `failed`, `out`, full) has a row for reads and a row for writes, destage or rebuild targets; each answer a copy can give (200, 404, 412, 503 by reason, transport error, no answer) has a row in the engine; each outcome of a PUT (committed, identical, conflict, abandoned copy, generation mismatch, too few copies, not definitive, throttled, rejected) has a row in the front; each way an object can lose a copy (stalled or failed replica, node loss, NVMe loss, drive loss, bitrot, GC, hot-only eviction) has a row that shows what restores it or that it becomes a miss; each decision two nodes could make differently (a seal, a descriptor, a free) has a row under partitions and lost messages; each ordering between a record, a relocate and a tombstone of one key has a row, including identical bytes re-PUT after a delete; each crash point of destage, conversion, rebuild and recovery has a scripted row; each boundary of a size rule (`large_min`, `block_bytes - block_reserve`, `seal_min_bytes`, the marks, `fill_limit` and the rebuild ceiling) has a row on both sides; and an object that is lost beyond the code is a miss, never wrong bytes. On the client side of revision 8: each component set is enumerated and either starts or is refused with its missing dependencies; each answer a seed can give (none, `404`, `401`, an invalid document, a valid one) and each epoch relation (higher, equal, lower) has a row.

## 15. How constants are frozen
Starting constants in sections 4, 5 and 10 are replaced by T0, T0-F, T0-S and T10b reports; a constant without a measurement behind it is marked [I] in the code comment that defines it.

## 16. Farm measurement plan (T0, T0-F and T0-S, before tuning)
Farm targets, never on a developer machine. On a build node, run on **one node drained of jobs** (no mounts, no Prepare) while the jobscheduler holds admission on the others; on a storage node, run before it holds data, or on drives that carry no data (scratch files go in `ts-scratch` directories on the drives' xfs, never in `ts-hot` or `ts-cold` of a live drive, never on silo's data). Before each run, record `iostat -x` of the drives involved for 60 s and abort if util > 5%. Each cell: 20 s after a 5 s warm-up, page cache dropped between cells; output MB/s, p50, p99, `iostat -x` (`rareq-sz`, `r_await`, `aqu-sz`, `util`). **Nothing here loads a kernel module or changes firewall or socket state on a node.**

**A. Raw drives (T0-S, on the storage nodes once they exist; one HDD, then all 15 at once; the NVMe).**
- *Facts, read only:* model, rpm, `lsblk -t -D -z` (zoned or not), `smartctl -i`, `xfs_info`, SCSI `timeout` and `eh_timeout`, scheduler, `nr_requests`, write-cache state; **CMR or SMR**, which decides whether the steady-state churn arm below runs in full.
- *HDD sequential:* request size {1, 2, 4, 8, 16 MiB} x streams {1, 2, 4, 8} x {buffered readahead, `O_DIRECT`} x {adjacent, scattered}; the same with all 15 drives busy (HBA and backplane ceilings). Decides `pos_ms`, `media_MBps`, `gap_cells`, and whether the cold tier uses `O_DIRECT` or buffered.
- *NVMe:* power-loss protection (from the vendor data and `nvme id-ctrl`, read only; without PLP the owner decides before T-S4 freezes `min_acks`, 10.3); sequential write MB/s; `fdatasync` latency distribution for 1 KiB to 1 MiB appends at 1, 4 and 16 writers, on an appended file, a `fallocate`d file and a pre-written file (group-commit batch sizes and the latency a small PUT sees); read latency p50 and p99 at queue depths {1, 4, 16, 64}; mixed 70/30; buffered versus `O_DIRECT` reads; `fadvise(DONTNEED)` effect. Decides `hot.queue_depth`, `batch_max`, the read path.
- *Network:* link speed (`ethtool`, read only) and a Rust bench target for throughput and latency storage-to-storage, build-to-storage, and 31 build nodes pulling at once (the fan-in), with and without erasure-coding CPU load.
- *CPU:* `reed-solomon-simd` encode and decode MB/s per core for `4+2`, `4+4`, `5+4`, `6+3` at 1, 2 and 4 MiB windows and 1 GiB blocks, including a parity-shard rebuild (restore data, re-encode); blake3 and crc32c MB/s; `sha256` MB/s per core with `sha_ni` on the build nodes. Decides encode threads, hash threads and `cpu.weight`.

**B. Cache device (build node).** On (i) the loop path (the first results are in the measured facts: `O_DIRECT` absorbed by the page cache, buffered writers stalled for more than 20 s), (ii) a file on the host root fs, (iii) the new partition once it exists: write block {128 KiB, 1 MiB, 4 MiB} x jobs {1, 4, 16} x {buffered + `fdatasync` per 32 MiB, `O_DIRECT`}; `fdatasync` latency at 1 MiB batched every 1 s; 70% 128 KiB random reads over 30% sequential writes; `PUNCH_HOLE` on 16 MiB groups and whether free space returns; DONTNEED arms (a) never, (b) at commit, (c) cap-driven, on a read-after-serve pattern. **Records `findmnt` of the root fs.** Records the partition's numbers for section 11 and decides the dirty cap, hash pool and DONTNEED default.

**C. The storage tier.** C-1, C-6, C-7 and C-10 need no tier code and run first (T0-F-a); the others run on the tier's own code (T0-F-b, T0-S).
- **C-1 silo baseline (legacy farm):** ranged GETs through the local silo, part size {4, 8, 16, 32, 64} MiB x concurrent GETs per node {1, 2, 4, 8} x active nodes {1, 6}; per-drive `iostat` (`rareq-sz`, kB read per GET byte, util).
- **C-2 one HDD streaming whole packs (storage node, prototype or tier code):** whole-pack and 64 MiB part reads, streams per drive {1, 2, 3, 4}, chunk {1, 2, 4, 8} MiB, depth {1, 2, 4}, slice {8..128} MiB, `O_DIRECT` versus buffered `sendfile` with DONTNEED; MB/s per drive, bytes per busy-second, p50 and p99 of a **1 MiB and a 4 MiB demand read** arriving at an already-busy drive with and without demand preemption.
- **C-2b mixed read and write on one HDD:** one shard-write stream and one read stream with demand arrivals at {0, 2, 8} per second, at `write_min_share` {0, 25, 50}%; decides `write_min_share` and confirms the class order.
- **C-3 hot versus cold from many clients:** 1, 6 and 31 build nodes fetching packs of {16 MiB, 256 MiB, 1 GiB, 4 GiB} from (i) the hot tier (either replica) and (ii) the cold tier (one shard); hedge to the other replica against a loaded drive; striping across hot replicas for packs over 128 MiB (4.4); network CPU. **C-3b promote:** a skewed read mix with and without promotion; hit rate, promo bytes, NVMe cost.
- **C-4 idle and loaded first byte:** GET of {1, 2, 4, 8} MiB at cluster concurrency 1 (decides the first-part size: smallest with idle first byte <= 40 ms for cold), and the same under the C-3 load (the thresholds of 4.6).
- **C-5 write path:** hot PUT latency and rate for 1 KiB, 64 KiB, 1 MiB records and 4 MiB to 1 GiB files at replicas 1 and 2 (the ack rule, group-commit batch distribution, one slow replica, seal-and-roll time); cold shard writes (1 GiB sequential, `O_DIRECT` 4 MiB depth 2, with and without the 64 MiB extent hint and `fallocate`; extent count per file; allocated bytes against file size) at 1 and 2 writers per drive; the whole destage pipeline for `4+2`, `4+4`, `replicas = 2` (stripes per hour, CPU, network); sustained write MB/s over 200 GiB per drive.
- **C-5b steady-state churn (only if C0 finds SMR; otherwise a short check):** a drive filled to 85%, then stripes freed by `unlink` and new shards written into the holes for **at least 3 drive capacities of writes**, with a read stream beside it; sustained write MB/s per drive capacity written, the ratio to a fresh drive, read p99, extent counts over time.
- **C-6 workload facts:** the object-size distribution of silo's objects split by kind (packs, tree lists, L2 sidecars, other): count and bytes per bucket {<= 64 KiB, <= 1 MiB, <= 4 MiB, <= 16 MiB, <= 256 MiB, larger}; `L_silo` and `L_live`; the explanation of silo's raw-to-logical gap; the identity of the requesters behind today's GETs and of the 147 DELETEs. Decides `large_min`, `block_bytes` and `destage.pack_window` (from the pack size distribution: first-fit waste for 400 to 700 MiB packs), confirms the capacity table (10.15) and the import volume (10.10).
- **C-7 placement:** distribution of the real key set under node rank; shard placement over 45 drives with zero, one and two nodes down (offline computation).
- **C-8 slow consumer:** two clients that stop reading mid-stream and a third that reads normally, against one HDD, with slot release on and off. Decides `slot_release_after`.
- **C-9 failing drive and startup:** read the kernel's per-device command and error-handling timeouts on every drive, and run the tier's injected hang (a test I/O layer that never completes a chunk) on real threads; a real failing-drive injection only if it can be induced without loading a module or touching a drive with data, otherwise reported as not run. **Index recovery:** reopen of the embedded store from its replay mark, open-segment replay (a 1 GiB segment of 1 KiB records), trailer reads of hot blocks, `blk/*.idx` load, and the cold-trailer rebuild (one read per block on a drive of 5.6 k shards, cold dentry cache), at 1.4 M and at a synthetic 100 M keys; bytes per key on disk and in cache, point-read p99 for `LOCATE`, bulk-load time, for `fjall` and `redb`.
- **C-10 GC replay (offline):** replay the death times of silo's packs (from the CAS service's data once its GC, or a synthetic min_ttl 7 d / action_ttl 30 d, has run) against (a) arrival-order stripes and (b) expiry-class stripes; report stripes freed whole, live fraction of the rest, rewrite bytes per byte freed at `rewrite_below` {0.2, 0.3, 0.5}, and `cold_fill_efficiency` over time (sealed block sizes at today's ingest with the age-seal floor, partial stripes, dead bytes).
- **C-11 erasure coding in operation:** aggregate cold read MB/s from 31 clients with one node down (the 2.8 GB/s estimate of 4.3); degraded read latency for ranges {4 KiB, 64 KiB, 1 MiB, 4 MiB} with one drive down and with one node down (`k` reads from `k` drives versus the healthy read), hedged and not; rebuild of a scratch drive (200 GiB of shards) at `rebuild.share` {10, 25, 50, 100}% with a foreground read load: rebuild MB/s, foreground p99; scrub rate and its foreground cost.
- **C-12 failure drills:** a storage node taken down mid-PUT-storm and mid-fill; a drive stalled by the injected hang; seal-and-roll under a slow replica; node return after `< node_down_after` (nothing rebuilt) and a node declared `out` (rebuild onto two nodes at `4+4`).
- Per run: aggregate MB/s, per-request p50/p99, first byte, per-drive iostat, CPU.
- Decides: **P**, `s*`, `per_drive_max`, chunk, slice, depth, `demand_preempt_max`, `write_min_share`, `slot_release_after`, `stall_after`, node floor, first-part size, thresholds in 4.6, `large_min`, `seal_max_age`, the marks and `destage` constants, `promote` constants, `rebuild.share`, the fill marks, the code default against capacity, striping default.

**D. CPU.** In A. **E. Node facts.** On every build node: `uname -r`, `fuse.enable_uring`, fuse module state, CPU count, `kernel.io_uring_disabled`, memlock limits, seccomp profile of job sandboxes, the FUSE request timeout, `vm.dirty_*`; **root filesystem type** and free unallocated SSD space; and, read only, **how the tree id is derived (T-FMT Q1)**. On every storage node: CPU model and count, RAM, NUMA, NIC speed, HBA and drive topology. Decides which build nodes run io_uring, the cache device plan, and the encode thread counts.

**Acceptance of T0, T0-F and T0-S (report, not code):** one table per section, the final constants, and any contradicted inference named as such.

## 17. Benchmark plan
The benchmarks below compare candidate FUSE libraries (fuser, fuse3/rfuse3, polyfuse, fuse-backend-rs) with `tsfuse`, and the storage tier with silo. Results are published with their raw data and configuration. Numbers come from **one farm node**, one harness throughout.

### 17.1 FUSE layer
**Harness.** `ts-fs-open-stall-load`: N open/read/close processes over a real komira tree (same tree and file list for every variant) plus CPU burners (one per core), 60 s per run, **at least 5 runs per variant, median and min/max**, first run discarded. Node drained. Per run record kernel, `fuse` module parameters, CPU governor and count, mount options, `entry_timeout`/`attr_timeout`, `max_read`/`max_write`, open mode, reader count, library commit. Harness and raw data live in a bench crate; no shell.

**Variants.** V1 fuser fork as shipped in 0.3.119 (baseline 2,769 opens/s, p50 74 ms, p99 312 ms; re-run on the day). V2 fork plus the pin fix of komira-ai/tierstore#343 (28,199 opens/s, p50 7.7 ms, p99 26 ms; re-run). V3 `tsfuse` `/dev/fuse`, 1 reader and N cloned readers (N in {2, 4, 8, cores/4}), writev replies. V4 same with splice replies. V5 `tsfuse` over io_uring, default depth and larger. V6 (optional, only if each costs under a day) a minimal read-only filesystem on fuse3 (rfuse3) and fuse-backend-rs over the same mapped list; polyfuse and the rest are a **static feature table** (async dispatch, clone, splice, uring, resend, copy count per read) read from source at pinned commits; unread cells stay empty.

**Two regimes, both reported.** (a) Library regime: `entry_timeout=0`, `attr_timeout=0`, explicit OPEN each time (the shipped baselines' settings). (b) Production regime: long timeouts and `NO_OPEN`. A **null filesystem** isolates transport from tierstore logic; V3-V5 run with both.

**Metrics.** opens/s; GETATTR and OPEN latency p50, p99, max (library histograms and client side); read MB/s cold and warm; **CPU per 1k requests** (server `utime+stime`, cgroup kernel time, whole-node delta); **D-state and FUSE waiting under a real komira build** (named targets): `fuse_d_state_threads`, `/sys/fs/fuse/connections/<n>/waiting`, D-state counts, per-request latency histogram, build wall time and FUSE-wait fraction; for V5 `uring_entries_parked` and `uring_registered_bytes` under a cold-read storm of `depth x queues x 1.5` and metadata p99 during it.

**Decision rules (before the data).** io_uring is default only if it improves CPU per 1k requests or p99 by at least 20% against `/dev/fuse` with clones, does not regress cold or warm read MB/s by more than 5%, survives the storm without metadata p99 growth, **fits 64 MiB of registered memory per mount**, and satisfies the takeover requirement (5.2 item 5). Splice replaces `pread`+`writev` above a threshold only where it wins on CPU per 1k requests at equal MB/s. DONTNEED stays on only if warm MB/s does not fall and page cache falls. The number of cloned readers is the knee of opens/s versus CPU; the shared pending queue (5.2) predicts a knee.

### 17.2 The storage tier versus silo (and a reference run)
Same harness, 5 runs, one table (T-B5). Silo measured on its own farm (C-1) and the storage tier on the storage nodes; where a like-for-like drive comparison is needed, the same HDD model is used or the difference is stated.
1. **One HDD streaming whole packs** (tier, 1, 2, 3 streams) versus **silo 8+4 ranged GETs** (8, 16, 32, 64 MiB at 1, 2, 4, 8 concurrent): MB/s per drive, bytes per busy-second, kB read per GET byte. **Hot tier** reads of the same packs from NVMe.
2. **Tail:** p50/p99/max of 1 MiB and 4 MiB demand reads under a background of {0, 2, 6} streams per drive: silo (waits for the slowest of 8 queues) versus the tier (one queue, preemption, hedge to the second hot replica or a degraded read).
3. **Pack fill end to end:** time to fill a 1 GiB and a 4 GiB pack into the node cache and tree-ready time for a real komira tree, at 1, 6 and 31 build nodes at once, from hot and from cold.
4. **Write:** 64 MiB and 1 GiB pack PUT at `hot.replicas` 1 and 2 against silo PUT; small-object PUTs at the measured size distribution (rate and p99); the destage rate; the cold sustained-write curve fresh and in steady-state churn (C-5b).
5. **Failure drills (reported here; no timing asserted in tests):** **one storage node down under a 31-node cold-start read storm, with a pass bar fixed before the run: aggregate cold read at least 2.5 GB/s (90% of the 2.8 GB/s estimate) and demand first-byte p99 at most 3 times the healthy p99 at the same load; a miss of the bar is reported and reopens the budget of 4.3 before T10b freezes constants**; a storage node down mid-fill; a drive stalled by the injected hang; degraded-read p99; rebuild time of a scratch drive and the extrapolation to a full one; fraction of keys with margin 0 and the audit histograms before and after.
6. **Reference run (optional, bounded to two days of a farm slot):** a single-node `weed server -s3` conformance run (create-only under concurrency, content ETag, ranged GET, prefix LIST, behaviour with the lock service down) and a two-node `replication 001` whole-pack read with and without vacuum on two idle storage nodes, replaying about a day of the measured PUT and GET mix with 1 GiB packs. It is a reference for 10.12, not a gate; it also validates the fallback of 10.12.

Raw CSVs and exact configuration are published with the result tables.

### 17.3 Does Prepare's order matter (T10b)
T10b reports, per build target over its jobs, the **first-touch hit rate** (`first_touch_total{ready="yes"}` over all first touches: a first read of a cell in a job session that found it `present`) and the **time to first read** (`first_read_seconds` p50 and p99), with Prepare's order of 6.1, beside the job's `read_wait_seconds`, `launch_before_ready_total` and amplification. These numbers decide whether the publish-time order hint of 6.1 is worth building: it is considered only if a material share of jobs waits on first touches that Prepare had not fetched yet. No test asserts any of these numbers (the measurement is a report).

## 18. Work breakdown (PR-sized, parallel, with interfaces, tests and start points)
Each task below is one or a few pull requests. **Starts** says when a task can begin: **PR up** means the moment this design is up for review, with no wait for any other task; "after X" names the tasks whose interfaces (on fakes) or results it needs. Each task's tests are the section 14 rows it makes pass (T10a holds the shared fakes), and each PR names them (rule T8).
Day-0 freeze: trait interfaces, the state page layout, the wire protocol, the store's on-disk and on-wire formats and the peer protocol land first with in-tree fakes, so every other task runs in parallel against fakes. The store's traits are frozen with the rules of section 10 already in them: content-derived generation, the cluster stamp in the record, relocate and tombstone formats and the liveness rule, the record, trailer, block, footer, descriptor, seal and free formats, create-only by the index under a key lock with the all-node check (`Index`, `HotStore`); the replication ballots, the majority seal and the ack rule (`HotStore`); the placement invariant and stripe descriptors (`Placement`, `ColdStore`); watchdog, scheduler liveness, read-ahead and slot release, write share, small lane, threads = streams x depth (`DriveSched`); `LOCATE`, per-copy `If-Match` and locations that carry generations (`Upstream`, `PeerClient`).

**T-I2: the freeze delta of revision 8.** Every change to an item T-I froze (komira-ai/tierstore#352), and whether it is additive:
- Operations (section 24): the stripe descriptor gains a blake3 per shard (a new optional field in its next version: additive, older readers ignore it), the `NODE` identity file gains the incarnation (additive), the header `x-ts-ledger-epoch` and the ledger fact record are new (additive), and `View` gains the ledger voters and per-component states of 24.2.
- `tierstore-store-api::view`: `Role`, `Role::stores` and `NodeView::role` are **removed** (decision 1: the document lists only nodes that run the hot store, so there is nothing to tell apart); `View::live_nodes` filters on the node's state and hot admin state only; `NodeView` gains `peer` (a `host:port` string) and an optional `s3` of the same form; the docs of `View::nodes` ("storage and combined; build nodes are not listed") and of `ClusterSettings::cold_code` (`cold.mode = "none"`) are reworded to "nodes that run the hot store" and "no cold store". `Placement::rank(key, view)` and every other reader of `View` (T-S4, T-S7) recompile unchanged. **Not additive at the Rust source level**; the crate is not published and its only users are this repository's tasks (T-S1, T-S6, T-S12, T10a), so no released binary or other repository sees it. The membership file is not yet shipped (T-S1 writes its parser), and its reader ignores unknown keys, so a document that still has a `role` key parses.
- `tierstore-store-api::proto`: a new route `MEMBERSHIP = "/store/v1/membership"` (GET; body the membership document as `application/toml`; `401` on a wrong token). The header `x-ts-mem-epoch` was frozen as "piggybacked on peer responses" and carried by `Envelope` on the typed answers, but a GET's `PeerBody` had no field for it; T-I2 states it is on every response of every route, the read routes included, and adds `PeerBody::mem_epoch`. **Additive on the wire**: no route, header or message changes meaning; a node of an older release answers the new route `404`, which a client treats as "try the next seed" (10.8). **At the Rust source level** `PeerBody` (all fields public, built by struct literal in the fake and in `tests/types.rs`, with a hand-written `Debug` that prints the new field) and `PeerClient` change: `PeerClient` gains `membership(node)` for following the epoch from a known node; bootstrap from a seed, which has an address and no node id yet, is a free function of `tierstore-client`, `fetch_membership(addr, token)`, returning the document's bytes and epoch. A wrong token is `PeerError::Protocol { status: 401 }` and a missing route `PeerError::NotFound`, so no new error variant is needed. Doc-only changes: `PeerClient`'s comment drops "a `combined` node", and the module doc of `proto` ("Build nodes use the read routes (`locate`, hot, cold and degraded GET, `ping`); storage nodes also use the internal routes") becomes "clients use `locate`, the hot, cold and degraded GETs and the membership route; nodes that run the hot store also use the internal routes and `ping`, which clients never send".
- `tierstore-store-api::proto`, load in `LOCATE` (10.6): `HotCopy` gains `load` (the copy's device queue wait in milliseconds as the answering node knows it, `Option<u32>`) and `LocateAnswer::Hot`'s copies are ordered by load with the first recommended (a meaning added to an existing order; promoted copies are no longer forced first); `ColdExtent` gains `drive_load` (`Option<u32>`) and its `sources` are chosen by the load table. A new header `x-ts-load` (`header::LOAD`) carries a node's load on internal routes, with a `NodeLoad` message type. Doc-only: `HotCopy::promoted` ("listed first") and `LocateAnswer::Hot::copies` ("a promoted copy first") become "ordered by load"; `tierstore-fetch-api`'s `ids.rs` rank doc ("profile order, else tree order") becomes "shared packs first, then tree order"; `PeerClient`'s doc stops quoting the section 14 row "View states on scripted pings". **Additive on the wire** (new optional JSON fields with defaults, a new header that older nodes ignore; an older node's answer has no load and its copies keep the old order, which a client reads as unranked). **At the Rust source level** `HotCopy` and `ColdExtent` are all-public structs built by struct literal, so the fake and the tests change with them.
- `tierstore-fetch-api::list`: `PACK_SHARED = 1 << 1`, a new meaning of a bit that was reserved zero, and the doc of `PackRecord::pack_flags` names both bits; additive (readers ignore unknown bits and do not mask them, 3.1). The two copies of `CELL_SHIFT = 20` (the state page's in `tierstore-fetch-api` and the block trailer's in `tierstore-format`) are unchanged.
- Unchanged: every format of `tierstore-format`, the state page layout, the mount protocol, `tsfuse`, the provider protocol (`provider.proto`), and every trait of `tierstore-fetch-api`.
Tests of T-I2: `tests/view.rs` rewritten (the role-name JSON cases and `only_storage_and_combined_roles_store_data` removed; live nodes are those up and not `out`; a membership document with an unknown `role` key parses), `proto` (the membership route on the fake node: `200` with the document and the epoch header, `401` on a wrong token; `mem_epoch` on a GET body), and `locate` (an answer with load fields decodes in a reader that ignores them, and an answer without them decodes with their defaults).

**Revision 8 and the tasks already running.** The decisions of revision 8 change these tasks of this table; the others are unaffected (their interfaces, crates aside, are unchanged):
- **T-S1** (spec and configuration): no `role` key and no `Role`; components as sections with the dependency check and validation item 8 of 10.14; `[client]`, `[cache]`, `[fetch]`, `[fuse]`, `[nfs]`, `[prepare]` sections; the membership document without roles and with addresses; the examples are files in `docs/examples/`.
- **Every client-side task below** places its code in the crate T-L creates; T-L's first PR (about a day) lands first, and a task already part-way through moves its modules into the crate unchanged.
- **T1, T1b, T8** (entry store, journal, eviction): code lands in `tierstore-cache` with documented public items, not in the daemon crate.
- **T2, T4** (hash pool, budget): code lands in `tierstore-client`.
- **T3** (fetch engine): split between `tierstore-client` (parts, retries, hedges, `LOCATE`, generation) and `tierstore-cache` (single flight, claims, `Engine`). No client pings: the client reads the recommended copy of the `LOCATE` answer, contacts nodes only in `rank(K)` order, keeps a client-local `suspect` from request outcomes, hedges at the p95 within a 5% budget, and a co-located client looks up its own index in-process (4.6, 10.6).
- **T5, T6** (mount protocol server and client): they are the shared cache's server and client in `tierstore-cache`; the protocol is unchanged.
- **T7** (planner): in `tierstore-tree`; ranks shared packs first, then tree order (6.1); no profile input.
- **T9** (metrics): the new metrics of section 12; per-crate registry handles.
- **T10a** (fake harness): the fake storage node serves the membership route (T-I2); the library-mode suite (T-L) uses the fakes.
- **T-FMT** (L2 format): readers ignore unknown `pack_flags` bits; bit 1 is `shared`; readers accept only `cell_shift = 20`.
- Not yet started and changed: T4 (controller signals from part responses and `LOCATE` answers, no pings), T-S6 (membership route, `StoreUpstream` in `tierstore-client`, load-ordered `LOCATE`, `x-ts-load` and the storage-node load table, pings between storage nodes only), T-S12 (step 5 of 21.3 is a configuration change, not a role change), T-F8 and T-M1 (adapters on `tierstore-tree`), T10b (the report of 17.3), T12 (on `tierstore-cache`).
- **Section 24 (operations and repair), not yet started and changed:** T-S9a and T-S9b keep the copy, decode, rebuild and scrub mechanics, and take their order, entry rule, throttle and commit protocol from T-S16's scheduler; T-S6 adds `x-ts-ledger-epoch` and the operator API's transport; T-S12's drain steps go through the membership validator (24.9); new: T-S13 to T-S21.
- Unaffected: T0, T0-F-a, T0-S, T-F0 to T-F7, T-F9, T-ENV, T-B1 to T-B4, T-J1, T-K1a, T-K1b, T-S0, T-S2 to T-S5, T-S7 to T-S11, T13, T14, T-R, T11a, T11b. (T-B5 reads through `tierstore-client`, which is where `StoreUpstream` lives in any case.)

**Estimate for the storage tier [E].** Revision 8's operations tasks (T-S13 to T-S21) add about 45 agent-days to the figures below, which predate them.  The T-S tasks sum to about 96 agent-days of code and tests (T-S0 to T-S12, T-S9 split into T-S9a and T-S9b) with a plan of about 95 and a range of **65 to 115, the upper half more likely**; the low end is the line-rate calibration of revision 5 (about 3 k lines per agent-day on plumbing PRs), the high end applies the usual two-to-three-times for crash-correct storage code. The CAS service tasks and benchmarks come on top (T-K1a, T-K1b, T-B5: about 10). Revision 5's estimate for its smaller cold tier was 25 to 40.

**Milestones (phased delivery, 10.13).** **S1** = T-S0 to T-S7 and **T-S9a**: hot tier, cold tier with `replicas = N`, S3 front with LIST, the repair `replicas = N` needs (re-replication of hot blocks and cold copies, scrub, margin and live-key audits). (There is no migration: silo was removed from the farm before the store was built, 10.10.) **On the NOW profile** S1 runs with `cold.replicas = 2` on the SMR drives (34.6 TB logical under the guard is about 26 TB at 2.0x, far above the 1.34 TB live) and S2 converts those blocks in place to `4+2` (10.5). **S2** = T-S8 and T-S9b: erasure coding (including the in-place conversion of S1's replicated blocks), rebuild by decode, deep scrub, the margin-0 rules. **S3** = T-S10: promote and GC rewrite; **T-S12** (the NOW-to-LATER migration) is needed when the storage nodes arrive and depends only on S2 (stripe freeing of fully dead stripes ships with T-S7's free protocol).

**Day 0 (no dependency):** T-I (every trait and format, the store's included: their rules are in section 10; merged as komira-ai/tierstore#352) and its delta T-I2, T0, T0-F-a, T-F0, T-B1, T-ENV, T-FMT Q1. **After T-I:** T-L's first PR (the three crates, about a day) ahead of the client-side tasks, then T-SB (on T-I2 and T-L), T-K1c (on T-FMT); T-S1 now starts after T-I2. **Right after T-I (about a day), in parallel against fakes:** T-FMT format, T-F1, T-F3, T-J1, T1, T1b, T2, T3, T4 (on starting constants), T5, T7, T8, T10a, T14, T-M1, and the store's T-S0, T-S1, T-S2, T-S5. **Then:** T-S3 (on T-S2), T-S4 (on T-S2 and the T-S3 interface), T-F2, T-F4..T-F9 (T-F5 and T-F6 on T-F0, T-F7 on T-ENV), T6 (on T5), T-K1a (on T-FMT). **Then:** T-S6 (on T-S3, T-S4, T-S5, T3), T-S7 (on T-S2, T-S5 and T-S6's interface), T0-S (first half as soon as the nodes exist; second half on T-S4, T-S5, T-S7, T-S8, T-S9b), T-K1b (on T-S6). **Then:** T-S9a (on T-S7), T-S8 (on T-S7), T-S9b (on T-S8, T-S9a), T-S10 (on T-S7, T-S6), T-S12 (on T-S8, T-S9b), T13, T-B2/T-B3/T-B5, T10b, T-B4, T11a (on T10b), T11b (done), T-R.

Longest chains: T-I -> T-S2 -> T-S3 -> T-S4 -> T-S6 -> T-S7 -> T-S9a (store, milestone S1: about 52 serial agent-days, the 57 planned with T-S11 less its 5 [E], and about 4 to 6 weeks more for S2 and S3 [E]) and T-I -> T-F1 -> T-F2 -> T-F8 -> T10b -> T-R (FUSE). The task graph has no cycle: T-S6 needs T-S3, T-S4 and T-S5 and not the CAS service; the CAS service's write side (T-K1b) follows T-S6.

**Which tasks start now, and which wait for this revision's formats.** Start now: T0-F-a, T0 (build nodes), T-F0, T-B1, T-ENV, T-FMT Q1, T-K1a, and the client-side tasks (T-F*, T1*, T2 to T9), none of which depend on the store's formats; the first half of T0-S as soon as the storage nodes exist (drive facts, NVMe `fdatasync` and PLP, network, RAM, encode and decode MB/s including a parity-shard rebuild). T-I freezes the store's record (stamp), tombstone, block, footer, seal and free formats and the replication messages (ballot, stamp) as written here; then T-S0, T-S1, T-S2 and T-S5 in parallel, and the rest in the order above.

## 19. Critique resolution

**The owner's decisions of revision 8**, and where each is applied.

| Decision | Applied as | Where |
|---|---|---|
| No roles in code | Components with sections and declared dependencies; any set whose dependencies are met is valid; `Role` removed from the frozen view; the deployment shapes are files in `docs/examples/` | 10.1, 10.8, 10.14, 21, T-I2 |
| The client as libraries, the daemon as a thin host | `tierstore-client`, `tierstore-cache`, `tierstore-tree` with versioned APIs, API tests and a library-mode suite; the daemon's fronts and Prepare use only them, checked by a layering lint; the client-side tasks mapped onto the crates | 23, 18 (T-L) |
| Learned access profiles (first asked for, then dropped by the owner as too ambitious for now) | Prepare's order is shared packs first, then tree order, under the whole-pack rule; eStargz and Nydus cited as prior art; a publish-time order hint in the L2 list named as a later option, gated on T10b's report | 6.1, 17.3, 18 (T-K1c) |
| Seed bootstrap of membership | Seeds or a file; `GET /store/v1/membership` with the cluster token; the epoch followed from `x-ts-mem-epoch`; an additive route of the peer protocol | 10.6, 10.8, 18 (T-SB, T-I2) |
| Explain the 1 MiB cell | Digest table, first byte, FUSE read size, fetch grid; `cell_shift` per pack | 2 |
| Two layers of reads, explained | FUSE reads of at most 1 MiB from the local cache; fetch parts of 16 to 64 MiB or whole packs from a storage drive into it | 4.0 |
| No client pings; load from the storage nodes; hedging; only `rank(K)` nodes; co-located `LOCATE` | Clients learn liveness from their own requests and the epoch from response headers; storage nodes share load on their own traffic (`x-ts-load`) and ping only when idle; `LOCATE` answers copies ordered by load with a recommended one; hedges at the p95 within a 5% budget; a client contacts nodes only in `rank(K)` order (past the key's replica nodes only to decide a miss) (a definitive miss walks further down the ranking); a co-located client looks up its own index | 4.3, 4.6, 4.9, 10.6, 10.7, 10.8, 14, 18 (T3, T4, T-S6, T-I2) |
| Operations, repair and runbooks | Section 24 (decisions D1 to D9), with every blocker of its adversarial review resolved (24.14) and the prioritized additions of 24.15; T-S13 to T-S21 | 24, 10.2, 10.4, 10.8, 18 |
| SeaweedFS's deciding gaps; `4+4` against two replicas | Fresh outputs from replicated NVMe here against HDD volumes or age-moved SSD volumes there; promote-on-read here, none for read-only EC volumes there; `4+4` is 2x raw like two replicas and buys tolerance, not capacity | 10.12, Q1 |

**The critique of revision 6** (an adversarial recheck of the storage tier; verdict: ready with named fixes) and the owner's rulings made after it. Each finding was resolved in revision 7 as below.

| Finding | Disposition | Where |
|---|---|---|
| F1 Tombstone ordering used a per-node counter: records and tombstones of one key written by different nodes were incomparable, and a re-PUT of identical bytes after a delete had the same `(key, generation)` as the deleted record | **Fixed.** A hybrid logical clock stamp, comparable across nodes, on records, relocates and tombstones; a writer asks every live node for the key before stamping a PUT or DELETE; the liveness rule is per key ("live iff no tombstone of the key has a larger stamp"), so identical bytes re-PUT after a delete are live; both failure cases are simulation tests. | 10.2, 14 |
| F2 Two sealers under a partition (the writer's early seal and a replica's take-over) had no tie-break; create-only was overstated during a view divergence | **Fixed.** A seal is a majority-committed record with a ballot (prepare, choose, accept), so at most one sealed length commits and later sealers adopt it; destage, re-replication and trailers act only on a committed seal; the simulation includes asymmetric partitions with a lost acknowledgement. Create-only is stated as linearizable when views agree, with divergence detected by `store_key_conflict_total`. | 10.3, 10.5, 14 |
| F3 Silo would be retired before the store could re-replicate a cold copy or a hot block | **Fixed.** T-S9 is split; T-S9a (hot and cold-copy re-replication, scrub of copies, margin and live-key audits) is in milestone S1, and silo's destructive step is gated on it; decode rebuild stays in S2 (T-S9b). | 10.11, 18 |
| F4 Degraded-read load was not in the budget | **Fixed.** A degraded part is `k` charges at the reconstructor and at the client; the reconstructor is chosen by queue; capacity with a node down is stated (about 2.8 GB/s); 17.2 item 5 has a pass bar. No read drain before maintenance (decided: the shards cannot be read elsewhere). | 4.3, 4.6, 10.4, 17.2 |
| F5 Rebuild arithmetic counted reads only and used a fill the guard forbids; survivors sat at `fill_limit` after a node-loss rebuild; margin 0 while a node is down | **Fixed.** Arithmetic counts writes and one decode per stripe, at the guard; the guard keeps one drive per survivor free; rebuild writes may reach `rebuild_fill_ceiling` (92%); while a node is down, scrub verifies margin-1 stripes first, margin-0 stripes rebuild first, and margin 0 alerts. | 10.4, 10.5, 10.15 |
| F6 Freeing a stripe had no owner or protocol | **Fixed.** The stripe's coordinator owns its free; it asks every node for per-block live counts; the free is a majority-committed record; shards are unlinked only after it; a re-PUT always writes a new hot record. | 10.5 |
| F7 Open segments per ordered pair and size class fragment the cold tier; partial stripes; objects just under a block | **Fixed.** One open segment per unordered replica pair and expiry class; age seal only above a 256 MiB floor; stripes from any pair, grouped by length; first-fit decreasing pack assembly; `block_reserve` makes the chain boundary exact; `cold_fill_efficiency` metric and C-10 replay. | 10.2, 10.3, 10.5, 16 |
| F8 The index was hand-rolled persistence over an unspecified in-memory map | **Fixed** by decision: an embedded ordered store (`fjall`, `redb` as the measured alternative), treated as a rebuildable cache with a replay mark; LIST seeks past rolled-up prefixes. | 10.2, 10.9, 18 |
| F9 Ceph RGW baseline missing; the SeaweedFS ratio fact; the decoder API | **Fixed.** Ceph RGW is a row of 10.12 with its reasons; the SeaweedFS ratio fact was re-read (default 10+4, per-volume shard counts in the current source, custom ratios Enterprise per the wiki) and no longer carries the decision; the decoder restores data shards only, and parity rebuild re-encodes. | 10.4, 10.12, 18 |
| F10 Reclaim order could evict a just-promoted copy; reject at 95%; `fdatasync` on appended files and PLP; NVMe as the failure unit | **Fixed.** No promotion above the destage-start mark; slow at 85%, reject at 90% (also of total fill); preallocation decided by T0-S; no PLP is a stop condition for the owner; NVMe loss is node loss for hot data, stated. | 10.3, 10.5, 10.13, 16 |
| F11 S1 replicated blocks would be re-copied to become EC | **Fixed.** In-place conversion: one copy is renamed as the data shard, only parity is written. | 10.5, 18 |
| Owner: tierstore owns the movement; destage decoupled from eviction; promoted copies separate | **Applied.** Destage copies dirty data; reclaim only drops clean data and never waits on a drive; cache segments are never destaged; the cold block stays authoritative. | 10.3, 10.5 |
| Owner: units explicit | **Applied.** Block for storage, placement, EC and destage; object or byte range for read, fetch and promotion. | 10.2 |
| Owner (after revision 7 was first proposed): cold code `4+4` and three hot replicas decided | **Applied** as decisions with their consequences (3x NVMe write fan-out, 6.4 TB usable hot, the sizing of 10.5 and 10.15, hot reads without a hop, the `N - min_acks + 1` miss rule). | Decisions, 10.2, 10.3, 10.6, 10.15 |
| Owner: one codebase for both deployments, cite published data or name the experiment, an implementation-ready breakdown | **Applied.** Section 21 (NOW and LATER profiles with full configurations, the migration by membership epochs), section 22 (evidence map), section 18 (tests and start point per task, T-S12). | 18, 21, 22 |
| Owner: a mode without a cold tier | **Applied.** No cold store (revision 7: `cold.mode = "none"`; revision 8: no `[cold]` section) with `hot.on_full = "evict" \| "reject"` and two example configurations. | 10.14 |
| T0 first results (drive rate, sha256 speed, loop device) | **Applied.** In the measured facts; sha256 via `ring`; the cache move is required. | facts, 4.7, 11, Q3 |
| Revision 5 claimed SeaweedFS's EC model was a reason to reject it | **Corrected.** SeaweedFS's EC already uses 1 GB contiguous blocks; the remaining reasons are listed in 10.12. | 10.12 |

**The evidence on SeaweedFS and on building (critiques before revision 6).**

| Finding | Disposition | Where |
|---|---|---|
| "Deletes constantly" is the wrong framing (147 DELETEs against 418,823 PUTs); the design's rejection rested on it | **Accepted.** Revision 5's argument is dropped; the reasons are those of 10.12. | 10.12 |
| "Refuted" overstates it; delete rate after GC is unknown | **Accepted.** GC volume is treated as unknown and sized for; the 38% dead bytes and 14,795 dead packs are the stated input. | Measured facts, 10.5, 10.13 |
| The capacity gap (silo raw about 4.6x logical) and long fill horizons are soft; 20x growth already happened once | **Accepted.** The new store does not depend on the gap; the horizon is given at today's rate and at 20x; C-6 explains the gap before the import volume is trusted. | 10.15, 16 C-6 |
| The workload counters do not measure the whole-pack load; the 74% create-only rejections are a load on the lock path | **Accepted.** Create-only is decided by the index under a key lock, no lock service; a measured pack-load run is in T0-S (C-3). | 10.5, 16 |
| "Loss is a miss" is a convenient framing for a store that holds the CAS data | **Accepted.** The tier is durable: no capacity eviction with a cold tier, redundancy per tier, audit of margin and unreadable keys; a pack lost beyond the code's tolerance is still a miss the CAS service republishes. | 4.6, 10.8 |
| Chunk fan-out is a configurable default; the dominant cost is hops and filer lookups; vacuum 2.3x is only at steady eviction; repair is scheduled | **Accepted and stated** in 10.12. | 10.12 |
| The filer store is the largest SeaweedFS operational unknown; the create-only lock couples availability to the lock service | **Accepted** and stated in 10.12. | 10.12 |
| Estimates are optimistic; segment reclaim is the same garbage problem; LIST is not 2 agent-days; perpetual ownership; rollback is not free; eviction coupling is data loss | **Accepted.** Estimate 65 to 115 agent-days with phased delivery; GC is designed and not claimed free; LIST is designed with an ordered index and merged fan-out; ownership and rollback are stated; capacity eviction does not exist with a cold tier. | 10.5, 10.9, 10.13, 18 |
| Run two cheap reversible experiments before committing | **Kept as an option:** the conformance run and the two-node reference run are T-S0 and 17.2 item 6, a reference and a fallback, not gates (the owner has decided to build). | 17.2, 18 |

**Revision 5's cold-tier findings (revision 4 critique, N-C1 to N-C11): disposition.** The cold tier of revision 5 (copies on the build nodes' SMR drives, HRW placement, per-file generations, eviction) is replaced, so most of its mechanisms are superseded rather than resolved; the lessons carry over as follows.

| Finding of revision 5 | Disposition (revisions 6 and 7) |
|---|---|
| N-C1 generation per PUT breaks create-only and failover | **Carried:** generation = blake3 of the bytes; create-only by the index under a key lock with the all-node check; conflict rule (10.2, 10.5). |
| N-C2 hung drive not detected; stuck thread not cancellable | **Carried** unchanged for HDDs: watchdog, `stalled`, probe before `up`, `failed` after three stalls (10.7). |
| N-C3 repair only on full reads; copy count decays unmeasured | **Replaced:** repair is rebuild by decode and scrub, margin and live-key audits (10.4, 10.8). |
| N-C4 node-wide reaction to one drive; slow consumer; third copy | **Carried:** per-drive permits plus a storage-node level (4.3); slot release (10.7); hot-spot defences (10.13). |
| N-C5 SMR churn; write class; fill marks | **Reduced:** writes are whole sequential shards and unlinks; the churn arm runs only if the drives are SMR; the write share is kept (10.7, 16 C-5b). |
| N-C6 tier eviction can fail a running job | **Removed by construction:** the store never evicts for capacity (10.5); the lost-mid-job path remains for DELETE and catastrophic loss (4.6). |
| N-C7 probe depth, hint, size not in name, `down` undefined | **Simplified:** three nodes, `LOCATE` in `rank(K)` order (revision 8: nodes in `rank(K)` order, load-ordered copies), index kept on `hot.replicas` nodes, definitive-miss rule `N - min_acks + 1` (10.6); states table kept (10.8). |
| N-C8 small objects have no policy | **Replaced:** hot-tier segments with group commit, sealed at 1 GiB, packed into cold blocks (10.3, 10.4). |
| N-C9 task graph cycle; T11 waits for silo | **Carried:** T-K1a and T-K1b split, T11a and T11b split, no cycle (18). |
| N-C10 migration capacity, rollback cost, destage race | **Changed:** no shared drives so no capacity inequality; rollback cost and import convergence stated (10.10). |
| N-C11 deletes without tombstones; space accounting; negative cache; threads versus depth; one EIO fails a drive | **Changed:** tombstone log ordered by the cluster stamp (10.2); space accounting by allocated bytes kept (10.4); negative cache and threads-versus-depth kept; EIO quarantines a shard (10.7). |

**Critique of revision 3: resolved in revision 4 and unchanged here.** F1 reply affinity and read-time cancel registration (5.2); F2 per-cell CAS eviction (5.1); F3 sealed state memfd, per-holder counter memfd, lifeline, post-read recheck (5.1); F4 takeover with every clone retained (5.2); F5 `durable` bit and server-only DONTNEED (4.8, 8); F6 id-preserving sidecar, dual emission first, node-by-node readers (3.5); F7 per-drive budget quantities and grid-relative ramp (4.3, 4.4); F8 macOS on the same engine (5.6); F9 io_uring memory, hybrid transport, environment (5.2); F10 INIT flags, splice length, pipe size, image page-in (5.2, 5.3); F11 separate runtimes (5.4); F12 T-ENV and dependencies (18); F13 boot id and batched punches (8); N2, N2b, N5, N11, S5, M1 with them.

## 20. Limits of this design
- **Nothing here was run, except the kernel checks of T-F0** ([tsfuse-kernel-findings.md](tsfuse-kernel-findings.md)). [I] and [E] items have named checks: T0-S and T0-F (drive behaviour, CMR or SMR, scheduler constants, network, encode speed, object sizes, GC replay), T-FMT Q1 (tree id).
- **The owner's decisions** are at the top: the hardware and the tier shapes, Q1 the cold code (`4+4`, decided), Q2 hot replicas (3, decided), Q3 per-cell digests (default yes, sha256), Q4 the old-tree trust gap.
- **The CAS service's decisions (T-K1a):** its 404 handling (the miss path), which the lost path of 4.6 relies on, and whether it sends the expiry-class hint.
- **Three storage nodes are a small cluster.** The tolerance is one node at a time (one spare shard at `4+4`, none at `4+2`); a second fault during a node outage or during the destage window loses data that is not yet protected twice. A minority partition can neither seal nor acknowledge. The design states the windows (10.13) and measures the margin (10.8); it does not claim more.
- **Create-only is linearizable only while the storage nodes' views agree**; a divergence is detected and counted, not prevented (10.5). With content-derived keys it converges.
- **Durability of hot data is `hot.replicas` copies until destage**; with `min_acks` below `replicas` it is weaker, as documented (10.3).
- **GC is designed and not measured.** Rewrite costs, the effect of expiry grouping and the promote thresholds are estimates until T0-F C-10 and C-3b.
- **The estimate is about 95 agent-days, 65 to 115 with the upper half more likely,** with a calendar of about 7 to 10 weeks to S1 and 4 to 6 more for S2 and S3 [E]. The fallback to an S3-compatible store (10.12) is kept available by the `Upstream` abstraction.
- **io_uring FUSE** (parked entries, memory, hybrid delivery, takeover) is decided by T-F0, T-F5 and the section 17 rules; `/dev/fuse` is the default until they pass. `fuse.enable_uring` is set at boot by the hostkeeper image.
- **Kernel facts** (reply matching per device, resend with clones, `F_SEAL_FUTURE_WRITE`, uring INTERRUPT delivery and the rest of 5.2 and 5.3) were checked by T-F0 on the build nodes' kernel, Ubuntu `7.0.0-42` and `7.0.0-43` ([tsfuse-kernel-findings.md](tsfuse-kernel-findings.md)): the `/dev/fuse`, page-cache, splice, memfd-write and DONTNEED facts by tests run there, the io_uring facts and the memfd mapping facts from that kernel's source only, until a node runs with `fuse.enable_uring=1` and AGENTS.md R2 allows their `unsafe` calls. `F_PUNCHHOLE` on APFS is T-M1's check.
- The CAS service's hashing CPU doubles (cell digests; T-FMT measured 325 MB/s per core for cells against 319 MB/s for whole files). The id-preserving backfill reads packs once, about 0.9 core-hours of hashing per TiB.
- The 60 ms cold first-byte figure is an idle-farm target; the loaded bound of 10.7 is a hypothesis for T10b.
- The first-part-small recipe, the P probe arm, striping across hot replicas, the write share, slot release, object-granular promote, expiry-class grouping and the per-stripe code in every shard footer are this design's own, with no external source; T0-S and T10b validate them. The majority seal is single-decree Paxos and the cluster stamp is a hybrid logical clock (References); their use here is new code with simulation tests, not a library.
- **The cold read capacity with a node down (about 2.8 GB/s) and the rebuild times are estimates** from vendor drive rates; T0-S and 17.2 measure them.
- **Whether Prepare's order matters is not known** until T10b's report (17.3); the shared-pack threshold is this design's guess.
- **The hedge budget (5% of parts, at the p95) is taken from published practice**, not measured here; C-3 and T10b check that hedges cut the tail without raising drive load beyond the budget.
- **Operations and repair (section 24) are designed, not run:** the timers, shares and the 14 to 20 hour node rebuild are estimates until the simulator, C-11 and the drills of 24.13 measure them; vendor defaults cited as comparators were read from their documentation and are not re-checked here.
- **The build system does not use the libraries yet:** its own rules admit no dependency on code of ours (section 23); the design does not depend on it.
- **The crate split moves code, not behaviour:** the client-side tasks keep their tests, and the API tests of section 23 freeze each crate's surface from 1.0; a later breaking change is a new major version.

## 21. Deployment profiles [D]
One codebase, one binary and one configuration schema (10.14) serve both deployments. A profile is only configuration (which components each machine runs, and their settings) and a membership document; nothing in the code branches on which profile runs, and the names "storage node", "build node" and "combined node" below name example files in `docs/examples/`, not anything the code knows (10.1). The store's formats are the same in both, and the code of every stripe is in its descriptor, so the migration from NOW to LATER moves bytes and changes configuration and membership, never code or formats.

### 21.1 NOW: six combined nodes on today's hardware
**Hardware [M]:** six nodes, each with 2 x 8 TB drive-managed SMR hard drives (ST8000DM004, xfs, which held silo until it was removed from the farm) and one SATA SSD of about 1 TB (root filesystem today; the node cache moves to a partition of it, section 11). There is no NVMe. Each node runs builds **and** stores packs: it runs every component of 10.1 except the NFS front, the configuration `docs/examples/now-combined.toml`.

**Hot tier: a partition of the SATA SSD, `hot.replicas = 2`, `min_acks = 2`.** The SSD is the only fast device. The hot tier gets its own xfs partition beside the cache partition (sized by T0 from the SSD's free space; this profile assumes 300 GiB [I]), so the two never share free space or a filesystem journal. Why two replicas, not three as on LATER:
- the SSD also holds the root filesystem and the build cache, so every extra copy is write load on the device the jobs read from; three copies of 34 GB/day is about 100 GB/day of SATA writes spread over six SSDs, which is affordable, but buys little here;
- with six failure domains, two copies already survive any one node, and the short destage window below bounds the time data spends on two devices;
- the consumer SATA SSDs have no power-loss protection [I, T0 records it], so each group commit is a real flash program (milliseconds [I]); at about 2.5 small PUTs per second that is acceptable, and fewer copies keep the tail short. The stop condition of 10.3 applies to the LATER NVMe only; on NOW the rate is low enough that T0's `fdatasync` latency sets `batch_max` and nothing else.
`LOCATE` asks the first live node of `rank(K)` (10.6); a hot read crosses the network five times in six, at SATA SSD speed, which is not the bottleneck: the co-located build cache absorbs repeats.

**Cold tier: `ec = 4+2` on the 12 SMR drives, failure domain node, one shard per node, `max_shards_per_domain = 2`.** Twelve drives on six nodes allow a code of 6 shards with one per node. `4+2` at one shard per node survives the loss of **any two nodes**, or of a node and a drive, at 1.5x raw; after a node is lost for good its stripes are rebuilt onto the five survivors with one survivor holding two shards (allowed, since `m = 2 >= 2`), so the cluster again survives a further node. Against the alternatives:
- `replicas = 2` costs 2.0x and survives only one node;
- `8+4` at two shards per node is also 1.5x and survives two nodes, but a stripe waits for 8 sealed blocks (more partial stripes at 34 GB/day, 10.5), a repair reads 8 shards instead of 4, and every stripe touches every drive;
- `4+4` would cost 2.0x for a tolerance six domains do not need.
`4+2` also keeps repair reads at 4, the same as LATER. On SMR drives the tier's write pattern, whole sequential shard files and whole-file unlinks, is the pattern drive-managed SMR handles well, and one healthy read is one stream on one drive [M: T0 measured 119 MB/s sequential and 36% of that at 1 MiB scattered on these drives]. C-5b (steady-state churn) runs in full on NOW because the drives are SMR.

**The drives' filesystems.** The tier's cold directories are `ts-cold/` on the SMR drives' xfs filesystems, which held silo when this was measured; silo has since been removed from the farm, so the figures below, which count its bytes, are a lower bound. Fill is measured on the filesystem (10.4), so any other bytes count: `0.85 x (96 TB - one node's 16 TB - one drive's 8 TB)` is 61.2 TB of filesystem use under the guard (`reserve_drives = 1`), of which silo holds about 9.3 TB [M], leaving about 51.9 TB raw, **about 34.6 TB logical at `4+2`**, against 1.34 TB live [E]. Silo's own I/O when this was measured (about 10 small writes per second per drive [M]), which the drive scheduler could see only through its service times, is gone with silo; the per-drive budget of 4.3 backs off on slow service times as on any slow drive.

**Configuration (NOW, node 1; the six nodes differ only in `node.id` and addresses):**
```toml
# docs/examples/now-combined.toml: components: hot store, cold store, peer service, S3 front,
# node cache, fetch engine, FUSE front, Prepare provider
[node]
id = 1
membership = "/etc/tierstore/membership.toml"      # six nodes, failure domain = node
token_file = "/etc/tierstore/cluster.token"

[peer]
listen = "192.0.2.21:7480"

[s3]
listen = "192.0.2.21:7481"
backend = "store"

[cache]
root = "/var/lib/tierstore-cache"     # the SSD cache partition (section 11)

[fetch]                               # finds the storage nodes through this process's own membership file

[fuse]

[prepare]

[store]
kind = "tiered"

[hot]                                 # SATA SSD partition
dir = "/var/lib/ts-hot"
replicas = 2
min_acks = 2
failure_domain = "node"
seal_bytes = "1023MiB"
seal_min_bytes = "256MiB"
dirty_destage_start = 0.40
dirty_destage_stop  = 0.25
dirty_urgent = 0.60
dirty_slow   = 0.80
dirty_reject = 0.90
promo_share = 0.10                    # the build cache beside it absorbs most repeat reads

[cold]
drives = ["/mnt/hdd1", "/mnt/hdd2"]   # the SMR drives' xfs; the tier writes under ts-cold/
block_bytes = "1GiB"
failure_domain = "node"
ec = { k = 4, m = 2 }
max_shards_per_domain = 2             # one per node at write time; two allowed after a node-loss rebuild
fill_limit = 0.85
node_loss_reserve = true
reserve_drives = 1

[destage]
min_age = "1h"
max_age = "1d"                        # short: the hot tier is two consumer SSD copies
partial_after = "3d"

[rebuild]
node_down_after = "24h"
share = 0.25

[drive]                               # SMR: one stream until T0-F says otherwise
streams = 1
write_streams = 1
```
Until milestone S2 ships erasure coding, NOW runs this profile with `cold.replicas = 2` in place of `ec` (about 26 TB logical under the guard against 1.34 TB live), and S2 converts those blocks in place (10.5). Validation passes: `4+2` on six node domains with at most two shards per node satisfies `m >= max_shards_per_domain`; `hot.replicas = 2` is at most six domains; the marks are ordered.

### 21.2 LATER: three storage nodes and 31 build nodes
**Hardware [D]:** 3 storage nodes of 1 x 6.4 TB U.2 NVMe and 15 x 6 TB SAS HDD; 31 build nodes with local SSD caches. **Hot tier: the NVMe, `hot.replicas = 3`, `min_acks = 2`** (Q2: every storage node holds every hot object; a hot read is served by the least-loaded node without a hop). **Cold tier: `ec = 4+4` at 3/3/2, failure domain node, `reserve_drives = 2`** (Q1). The storage node's configuration is the first example of 10.14 (`docs/examples/later-storage.toml`). A build node runs the node cache, the fetch engine, the FUSE front, the S3 front as a caching proxy and the Prepare provider, and no store component; its configuration, with every key of those sections commented, is below. Capacity is in 10.15.

```toml
# docs/examples/later-build.toml: one of the 31 build nodes of LATER; they differ only in addresses.
# Components: node cache, fetch engine, FUSE front, S3 front (caching proxy), Prepare provider.
# No [node] section: a node that runs no store component has no node id.
[client]                              # how tierstore-client finds the storage nodes (10.8)
seeds = ["192.0.2.11:7480", "192.0.2.12:7480", "192.0.2.13:7480"]   # or: membership = "<file>"
token_file = "/etc/tierstore/cluster.token"

[cache]                               # tierstore-cache (sections 5, 7, 8, 11)
root = "/var/lib/tierstore-cache"     # the SSD cache partition (section 11)
budget = "185GiB"                     # section 7
shared_socket = "/run/tierstore/cache.sock"   # the default; FUSE mount processes and other processes attach here (5.4, 23)

[fetch]                               # the fetch engine (section 4); its constants are frozen by T10b

[fuse]                                # tsfuse (5.2); the transport follows fuse.enable_uring, read at startup

[s3]
listen = "127.0.0.1:7481"
backend = "cache"                     # a caching proxy of the storage cluster (section 9)

[prepare]                             # the jobscheduler mount provider (section 6)
socket = "/run/tierstore/provider.sock"
```

### 21.3 Migration from NOW to LATER, without a rewrite
Each step is a membership or configuration change of the same binary; each is reversible until the last.
1. **Bring up the storage nodes dark:** format their drives (a held script per node), T0-S, start the daemons with the LATER storage configuration in a membership epoch where they are present and **`in`**, while the combined nodes stay `in`. Their indexes start empty and are filled by `index-sync` from the combined nodes; until then they answer `indexing`.
2. **Move new writes:** a new membership epoch marks every combined node's hot device and drives **`drain`**: they are no longer placement targets for hot replica sets or new stripes, but they keep serving reads. Replica sets are computed over nodes whose hot device is `in`, so from this epoch every PUT goes to the three storage nodes with `hot.replicas = 3`, and every new stripe is `4+4` on them. (The cluster-wide hot settings, `replicas = 3` and `min_acks = 2`, take effect in the same epoch.)
3. **Drain the hot tier of the combined nodes:** each seals its open segments (the majority seal of 10.3 now runs over all live nodes) and destages every dirty block onto the storage nodes' drives as `4+4` stripes; clean and cached copies are dropped. Nothing is copied to the storage nodes' NVMe: those blocks are cold.
4. **Drain the cold tier of the combined nodes by re-encoding:** for each `4+2` stripe with shards on `drain` drives, a coordinator on a storage node reads its four data shards (one sequential stream per SMR drive), writes four data and four parity shards at 3/3/2 on the storage nodes, commits the new descriptor (the same majority commit as destage; block ids and offsets are unchanged, so no index entry moves), and then unlinks the six old shards. It is a `background` job at `rebuild.share`, verified like a destage. At today's volume (a few TB of cold data) it is hours [E].
5. **Remove the store components:** when no data remains on `drain` devices (`cold_stripes{...}` and `hot_dirty_bytes` on the combined nodes at zero), a final epoch marks the combined nodes' hot devices and drives `out` and drops them from the document, and each combined node's configuration (the NOW example's name) loses `[node]`, `[store]`, `[hot]`, `[cold]`, `[peer]` and their settings sections (`[destage]`, `[rebuild]`, `[drive]` and any `[index]`, `[promote]`, `[gc]`), gains `[client]` with the storage nodes as seeds, and switches `s3.backend` to `"cache"`: it is now `docs/examples/later-build.toml`, one of the 31 build nodes. Their SMR drives are then freed.
**Rollback:** until step 5, marking the combined nodes `in` again restores them as placement targets; stripes already moved stay valid where they are, because a stripe's code and placement are in its descriptor. **Tests:** the migration is a scripted simulation in section 14 (the membership epochs above on fake nodes: reads succeed at every step, nothing is placed on `drain` devices, every stripe ends `4+4` on the storage nodes, no index entry changes during the re-encode).

## 22. Evidence: what published data settles, and what our experiments decide
The problems this design solves are largely solved elsewhere; where the published data is clear this design follows it, and where it is not, the experiment that decides it is named.

| Choice | Settled by | Or decided by |
|---|---|---|
| Small objects appended to large append-only files with an in-memory or embedded index; one disk operation per small read | Haystack (OSDI 2010) | C-5 (group-commit batch sizes), C-9 (index) |
| Seal at about 1 GB, then erasure-code lazily; hot copies dropped after the code is durable | f4 (OSDI 2014), Windows Azure Storage (SOSP 2011; seal at about 1 GB per the LRC paper) | C-5 destage rate, C-10 fill efficiency |
| Contiguous data blocks, so a healthy read touches one drive; parity read only on failure | f4; HDFS-7285 and HDFS-8030 (striping exists for small files and parallel bandwidth, which the hot tier's packing already provides) | C-2 (one drive streaming whole packs), C-3 |
| Range-granular degraded read; hedged reconstruction when a read is slow | f4 (reconstruct only the requested range); Windows Azure Storage / LRC (parallel reconstruction of slow reads) | C-11 and the 17.2 one-node-down pass bar |
